Hybrid Bypass Simulation

Hybrid Bypass Routing and Wavelength Assignment algorithm for traffic requests on 2 queues , having the requested capacities randomised by following a Uniform distribution process.

Implementation by Konstantinos Delistavrou © 2021-2025

Run configuration
RunID: 026e5bec-3bf4-4d97-9952-1541a800a335
argument use value(s)
argv[0] program file name codeHybridBypass.py
argv[1] network definition text file name N6L8_STnet_AllWavConv.txt
argv[2] index of average traffic load array 10, i.e., 60 Gbps
argv[3] set the name of the experment TestCase
argv[4] execution report details detailreport
argv[5] generate random traffic demands or load them load
argv[6] traffic demands text file name TR_STnet_6N_Uniform_30-70.txt
argv[7] output execution report as pdf nopdf
argv[8] traffic demand queues (number) 2
argv[9] keep the report and other generated files after the execution of the program keepreport
argv[10] name of the computer that execution takes place Gus
argv[11] program folder path under: the root folder (on MS Windows), or the home folder (on GNU/Linux) C:\simLightRun
argv[12] distribution of random traffic loads Uniform
argv[13] scheduling strategy Hybrid2Q
argv[14] number of fibers per link (f) (number) 1
argv[15] number of wavelengths per fiber (W) (number) 8
argv[16] wavelength capacity (C) (Gbps) 40
argv[17] IP router port latency (Lr) (microsecond) 30
argv[18] WDM transponder latency (Lt) (microsecond) 100
argv[19] traffic share percent of QHP (percent) (traffic of QLP is 100-argv[19]) 30
argv[20] consider traffic grooming rejection criterion (TGRC) Yes
argv[21] hard latency cap threshold for QHP (microsecond) -1.0
argv[22] hard latency cap threshold for QLP (microsecond) -1.0
argv[23] virtual wavelength capacity (VWC) (Gbps) -1
argv[24] wavelength converter latency (microsecond) 100
argv[25] wavelength converter power consumption (Watt) 50
argv[26] number of shortest paths to consider during wavelength assignment 9
Simulator output details
stdoutOriginal: <_io.TextIOWrapper name='' mode='w' encoding='utf-8'>
sys.stdout: <_io.TextIOWrapper name='C:\\simLightRun\\Alg_HybridBypass_Net-STnet_N6L8_AllWavConv_X-60_y2026_m9_d12_h12_m20_s57_u349084\\Alg_HybridBypass_Net-STnet_N6L8_AllWavConv_X-60.html' mode='a' encoding='utf-8'>
graphsPath: C:\simLightRun\Alg_HybridBypass_Net-STnet_N6L8_AllWavConv_X-60_y2026_m9_d12_h12_m20_s57_u349084
Maximum fiber capacity
Each wavelength capacity is 40 Gbps
Each fiber can multiplex up to 8 wavelengths
Therefore, the maximum fiber capacity can reach 320 Gbps
Maximum link capacity
Each link can include up to 1 fibers
Therefore, the maximum link capacity can reach 320 Gbps
The links are bidirectional. Therefore, each fiber can transfer information in both directions.
N: Set of nodes
Node 0 is Node0 with wavelength converters
Node 1 is Node1 with wavelength converters
Node 2 is Node2 with wavelength converters
Node 3 is Node3 with wavelength converters
Node 4 is Node4 with wavelength converters
Node 5 is Node5 with wavelength converters
Nm: Set of neighbouring nodes
Node 0 neighbours [1, 2]
Node 1 neighbours [0, 2, 3]
Node 2 neighbours [0, 1, 4]
Node 3 neighbours [1, 4, 5]
Node 4 neighbours [2, 3, 5]
Node 5 neighbours [3, 4]
L: Set of bidirectional Links
[[0, 1], [0, 2], [1, 2], [1, 3], [3, 5], [2, 4], [3, 4], [4, 5]]
G: The physical topology
(['Node0', 'Node1', 'Node2', 'Node3', 'Node4', 'Node5'], [[0, 1], [0, 2], [1, 2], [1, 3], [3, 5], [2, 4], [3, 4], [4, 5]])

The Physical Topology

PhysicalTopology.html


λ: Traffic demand per node pair (requests) in Gbps xi= 10 , X[ 10 ]= 60 for a total of 2 queues.
The traffic demand between each node pair is random following a Uniform distribution, which is centered at an identical average.
